Let's formalize multiplicative update (MU) for target u:
Given p>0 sum1, q=conv(p,p), K=2n-1.

Wait for update p_i_new = p_i / s_i where s_i = ∑_m u_m (∂q_m/∂p_i)/q_m. ∂q_m/∂p_i = 2 p_{m-i} if 0≤m-i<n, else 0. Thus s_i = 2∑_m r_m p_{m-i}. The extra 2 cancels in normalization. So s_i = ∑_m r_m p_{m-i} (convolution/correlation). Update p_i ← p_i * (1/s_i)^α, then normalize. α damping (0,1]. This is reminiscent of iterative proportion fitting for bilinear constraints.

For flatten update:
```
def flatten_update(p, gamma, damp, q_floor, max_ratio=None):
    n=len(p); K=2*n-1
    q=conv_full(p)
    qf=q+q_floor
    if gamma ==0:
       u = np.ones(K) / K
       r = u / qf
    else:
       logu = gamma * np.log(qf)
       logu -= logu.max()
       u = np.exp(logu); u/=u.sum()
       r = u / qf
    s = cross_corr(p, r)
    s = np.maximum(s, 1e-300)
    # log-update with damping
    # We want p_new ∝ p * s^{-alpha}. To avoid huge, center logs:
    logp = np.log(p) # p positive
    log_s = np.log(s)
    # multiply by -alpha; add constant? Normalization later, can subtract mean weighted? Maybe subtract np.mean(log_s)*alpha for stability
    logp_new = logp - damp * log_s
    logp_new -= np.max(logp_new)
    p_new = np.exp(logp_new); p_new/=p_new.sum()
    # floor
    p_new = np.maximum(p_new, min_p)
    p_new /= p_new.sum()
    return p_new
```
But if r huge due to q_floor, s could huge; damp small. Use q_floor = 1e-12 / K? q ~1/n~1e-4. If floor 1e-12, r~1e8 for edges; s ~ p * sum r maybe huge; log_s ~20. damp=0.01 gives change 0.2, ok. 2. For each base candidate p, run a "polish" function:
   - For several gamma values (0.0, 0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7), do 20-200 flatten steps with decreasing alpha. Actually gamma=0 (uniform) may be strongest; gamma>1? no.

### Multiplicative flatten update
```
def flatten_step(p, gamma=0.5, alpha=0.1, q_eps=1e-12):
    n=len(p)
    if n==0: return p
    K=2*n-1
    q=conv_self(p)
    qf=q+q_eps*n  # maybe
    if gamma <= 0:
        # target uniform: r=1/(K*qf)
        r = 1.0 / (K * qf)
    else:
        # u ∝ qf^gamma; r=u/qf
        # To avoid overflow, use log:
        loguf = gamma * np.log(qf)
        loguf -= loguf.max()
        u = np.exp(loguf)
        su = u.sum()
        r = (u/su) / qf
    s = cross_corr_p_w(p, r)
    # The desired p_new ∝ p / s^alpha? Need sign? Earlier uncertain. Let's derive again for minimizing variance? We need choose update direction empirically. We can implement both sign options and choose based on score. That is safest: for each alpha, try p/a s^alpha and p*a s^alpha, see score. But can be slow. Maybe use `direction` parameter.
```
What sign is correct for reducing max? Let's test intuitively with active peak subgradient: q_m high. t_i=∑w p_{m-i}. To reduce q, decrease p where t high -> p/s (negative). So for peak/high weights, p/s. For union low-weight target, sign maybe opposite. We can implement an `sgn` in multiplicative: `p_new = p * s^{-alpha}` (reduce high s) or `p * s^{alpha}` (increase high s). Evaluate and keep better. Since we can call score, we can choose adaptively per step: try sign=-1 (p/s) first because reduces peaks; if not improve, try sign=+1 (increase) maybe target edges. But doing two steps doubles time. Could try small alpha and if score not improve, flip sign. But sign may need flip for different modes.

Maybe we can use the LSE peak direction (p/s high) for high q weights, and a separate "edge filling" with low q weights but p*s? Let's understand target low q. To raise low q, we should increase p where it contributes to low q. For low lag m (edge), contribution to p0 maybe; low weights w_low, the derivative of q_low wrt p_i positive. To raise q_low, increase p_i where w_low p_{m-i} high. That is p*s (increase high s). So if we choose weights emphasizing low q (r ∝ 1/q), use positive sign to raise them, but this may also raise peaks? The objective is balance. We can define a weight vector `w` and sign:
- To decrease high q: weights high q, sign negative.
- To increase low q: weights low q (or 1/q), sign positive.
A balanced min-max subgradient uses weights high q only; it doesn't directly increase low q, but by moving mass away from peaks, low q may rise due to sum constraint? Actually transfer from peak contributors to others raises some lags. So sign negative with high-q weights might be enough.

Thus maybe focus on `peak_step` with LSE weights (high q) and sign negative. That is safer: always reduce current peaks. But from uniform, high-q weights symmetric -> t constant, no move. Parent not uniform. To break symmetry, use random perturbations or low-q sign positive.

We can implement both:
```
def weighted_mp_step(p, weights, alpha, sign):
    t = cross_corr(p, weights) # weights sum1
    t = t + 1e-300
    logp = np.log(p+1e-300)
    # center by weighted mean to control scale
    mean_t = np.dot(p, t)
    delta = alpha * sign * (t - mean_t)
    # clip delta to [-0.5,0.5]
    delta = np.clip(delta, -0.5, 0.5)
    p_new = p * np.exp(delta)
    p_new = np.clip(p_new, min_p, None); p_new/=p_new.sum()
    return p_new
```
For `sign=-1`, reduce where t high (peak contributions). For `sign=+1`, increase where t high.
Weights:
- LSE high: `w=softmax(beta*(q-M))`.
- Edge/low: `w=softmax(-beta*(q-M))` (emphasizes low q). For sign + maybe.
But weights sum1; if q range small, beta needs O(n). We can choose beta adaptive: beta = c / max(1,n*(M-min_q))? Let's compute q max M, min maybe 0. The range R = M - min(q). For n=7000, R~1.5e-4. To get exponent 1, beta=1/R~60000. To get active top, beta several/R. We can set `beta = target_range / R` with target_range e.g. 1,2,5. But q may have many near zero; if R=0 (uniform?), fallback.

How to compute weights efficiently without computing q twice? In step, compute q for score and weights. We can combine.

```

Let `g = (t - mean_t) / (np.std(t)+eps)`, delta=eta*g. Then eta O(0.1). This scale-invariant. But if we use p*s with t, the fixed point multiplier uses raw s; scale matters. Implement:
```
g = t - np.dot(p,t)  # weighted mean approx M
g_rms = sqrt(np.mean(g**2)) or norm; if g_rms>1e-30: g = sign* eta * g / g_rms
clip g ±0.1? 
p_new = p * exp(g)
```

### L-BFGS on softmax LSE
This may converge faster near optimum. Let's implement robustly but limited.
```
def softmax(x): m=np.max(x); e=np.exp(x-m); p=e/e.sum(); return p

def lse_obj_grad_x(x, beta):
    p=softmax(x)
    q=conv_self(p)
    M=q.max()
    scaled=beta*(q-M); clip; w=exp(scaled); w/=w.sum()
    L=M+np.log(w.sum())/beta (but w normalized logsumexp)
    score=2*n*L
    t=cross_corr(p,w)
    grad_p=2*t
    grad_x=p*(grad_p - np.dot(p, grad_p))
    return score, grad_x
```
We can wrap for `minimize`. Since helpers top-level, define `lse_x_obj(x, beta)` and `lse_x_jac(x, beta)`; but `minimize` with `jac=True` needs function returning (f,g) or jac. Top-level functions can take args.
```
def lse_x_obj(x, beta): return lse_obj_grad_x(x,beta)
```
Use `minimize(lse_obj_gradient, x0, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, options=...)`. But our function returns tuple? L-BFGS-B expects if jac=True, function can return (f,g). In scipy >=1.11? It supports. Or define separate:
```
def lse_x_f(x,beta): return lse_obj_grad_x(x,beta)[0]
def lse_x_g(x,beta): return lse_obj_grad_x(x,beta)[1]
```
But this recomputes. Use cache? No closures. Could define a top-level object? Simpler return tuple; SciPy accepts `fun` returning (f,g) if jac=True? I think yes in recent. To be safe, implement separate using a global cache? Top-level dict keyed? Not thread. Could use `fun` returning f and `jac` separate, but double function eval. L-BFGS calls with `fun_and_grad`? Modern SciPy has `fun` can return tuple when `jac=True`. We'll assume okay. If not, maybe use `minimize(..., jac=lse_x_jac)` but then objective recomputed. We can create a class? Closures not allowed but class top-level okay? Simpler:
```
def lse_x_f_and_g(x, beta):
    return lse_obj_grad_x(x,beta)
```

```
def causal_sqrt(u, n):
    # u length at least n? recurrence for k=0..n-1 needs u_k.
    r=np.empty(n)
    r0=np.sqrt(u[0]) if u[0]>0 else 1e-3
    r[0]=r0
    for k in range(1,n):
        # cross = sum_{i=1}^{k-1} r_i r_{k-i}
        if k>=2:
            cross = np.dot(r[1:k], r[k-1:0:-1])  # length k-1, sum r_i*r_{k-i}; for i=1..k-1 includes both pairs? Wait earlier formula ∑_{i=1}^{k-1} r_i r_{k-i}. This sum includes each unordered pair twice if i≠k-i? Let's re-derive: R^2 coeff k = ∑_{i=0}^k r_i r_{k-i}. Isolate i=0 and i=k: 2 r0 r_k. Remaining i=1..k-1: ∑_{i=1}^{k-1} r_i r_{k-i}. For k=3, i=1,r1r2; i=2,r2r1 => sum includes twice. Yes cross=dot(r[1:k], r[k-1:0:-1]) gives r1*r2 + r2*r1 =2r1r2. Good.
        else cross=0
        r[k]=(u[k] - cross)/(2*r0)
    return r
```

### Can we directly solve the recurrence for p from the **actual** q target via Newton?

### A more precise local optimizer: trust region with active q and linear algebra
Maybe we can solve for a **multiplicative fixed point** with weights from dual KKT. The KKT condition: There exists active weights λ sum1 such that t_i= M for p_i>0. We can find λ by iterative projection:
- Initialize λ uniform over active lags (current top where q close M).
- Compute t_i. We want t_i approximately constant over support. Since p fixed, t_i linear in λ. We can adjust λ to reduce variance of t_i on support. This is a nonnegative least squares problem: minimize ||Cλ - M 1||^2 + regularization, λ≥0, sumλ=1. C rows are p_{m-i} for active m. Matrix C (n x A). If A small (e.g., top 200), we can solve using `scipy.optimize.nnls` or regularized least squares. Then use λ as weights for a subgradient step (or update p with p*=exp(-η(t-M))). This is more tailored than LSE.
Could also update p and λ alternately:
   1. Given p, compute q, active A (top lags near M). Solve for λ that equalizes t on support (maybe all i). This gives KKT direction.

Detailed `age_step(p, alpha, active_frac=0.02)`:
- q, M. active_idx = top `A = min(200, max(20, int(n*active_frac)))` where q largest. Maybe include all with q > M - max(1e-6, 0.001*M).

- ratio = mean_t / (t + eps); log_step = alpha * np.log(ratio); clip; p_new = p*exp(log_step); normalize floor.
- Score new. Accept if better; else reduce alpha.

Polish function top-level:

Polish procedure:
1. `p = np.clip(p, min_val, None); normalize`.
2. Compute score; set best.
3. Loop until time slice:
   a. `q = conv_self(p); M=q.max(); score=2nM`.
   b. If near timeout break.
   c. Choose method cycle: maybe every 20 iterations AGE, every 50 L-BFGS? But L-BFGS as separate phase.
   d. Generate step with alternating:
       - active gradient equalization (AGE) using top active weights, update variants, line search.
       - LSE peak multiplicative with beta schedule.
       - flatten low-MU? maybe.
   e. Accept best improvement; if no improvement for many, reduce step, maybe perturb.
4. After loops, optional L-BFGS on softmax for a few betas using remaining time.
5. exact peak shave with top active (single subgradient) small step.
Return best.

But too many methods can be complex. Simplify to a robust loop with a few step types:
- `low_mu_step` (target uniform) maybe not sure sign.
- `peak_step` with LSE weights and p/s.
- `age_step`.
We can try each in sequence and line search; keep best.

Given time, perhaps implement `multiplicative_peak` as main, and AGE occasionally. L-BFGS final.

Line search function:
```
def line_search_step(p, old_score, step_func, step_params, max_eval=5):
    best=p, best_score=old_score
    alpha=current
    for k in range(max_eval):
       p1=step_func(p, alpha)
       sc=exact_score_p(p1)
       if sc < best_score - 1e-15:
           return p1, sc, alpha
       alpha *= 0.5
    return best, best_score, alpha*0.25
```

### AGE step details
`age_factor(p, alpha, n_active=100)` returns p_new or None:
- q=conv_self(p); M=q.max()

- active_idx = np.argpartition(q, -A)[-A:] maybe. We can use `np.argsort(q)[-A:]` for small.
- Build C (n x A) loop.
- Try `lam, res = scipy.optimize.nnls(C, M * np.ones(n))`. `nnls` may be slow; use only occasionally (e.g., every 20 loops). If fails, lam = ones(A).
- lam /= sum.
- t = cross_corr(p, lam)
- mean_t = dot(p,t); floor t.
- p_new = p * np.exp(alpha * np.log(mean_t / (t+eps)))  # p/ t^alpha if alpha positive? Since log(mean/t) positive where t low. This increases low t, decreases high t, making p*t? It targets t constant if p unchanged. Use alpha 0.1.
- Maybe alternative p_new2 = p * np.exp(-alpha*(t-mean_t)/(rms)) for Euclidean. We can test both and pick lower score inside AGE? That doubles. Maybe choose log ratio (multiplicative) because position.
- Normalize floor.
Return p_new, q, M maybe.

### Causal sqrt seeds
`causal_sqrt_seed(n, target='uniform', shift=0.0, decay=0.0, mix=1.0)`:

- if mix between 0 and1: r2 = r[::-1]; p = mix*r + (1-mix)*r2. But if r tail negative? use positive r.
- normalize p.
Return p.

Let's design `polish_sequence` in detail.

```
def polish_sequence(p, budget_s, rng, verbose=None):
    n=len(p); p=normalize_p(np.clip(p,0,None))
    best_p=p.copy(); best_score=score_p(p)
    alphas = {'peak':0.2, 'low':0.05, 'age':0.05, 'lbfgs':...}
    fails=0
    last_improve=time
    loop_count=0
    t_end=time.monotonic()+budget_s
    while time < t_end:
        loop_count+=1
        # choose method
        if loop_count % 20 == 0 and n<=15000 and time...:
            p1 = age_step(p, current alpha_age, n_active=80)
            sc1=score_p(p1)
            if improves: p=p1; best...; alpha_age*=1.05; fails=0
            else: alpha_age*=0.7
        elif loop_count % 10 == 0:
            # low weight flat step sign + maybe
        else:
            # peak LSE step
        # occasionally perturb if no improve 30 loops
        if fails > 30:
            p = random_perturb(p, rng, scale=0.01)
            fails=0
        # Check if best score equal? maybe
    # final small coordinate? 
    return best_p, best_score
```
Need `score_p` uses conv; `age_step` also computes q; to avoid double, age_step can return new_p and old_score? But we need p score for current? We can compute q once in loop and pass to age? Top-level functions can accept q. But simpler maybe okay.

To reduce FFTs, implement `peak_step_with_q(p, q, beta, alpha)`:
- weights from q.
- t=cross_corr; p_new; return p_new.
Loop computes q/score once, then generates candidate(s). Candidate score computed by conv. So 2 conv per accepted attempt. Good.

`age_step_with_q(p,q, alpha, n_active)` can use q for active and M. Needs t via cross_corr. 1 additional FFT for t + candidate score conv.

`peak_step_with_q` similar.

`lowflat_step_with_q(p,q, alpha)` maybe uses weights low and positive sign.

We can alternate:
- Compute q, M, score.
- Generate peak candidate with beta schedule (beta increases over loops). If improves, update; else reduce alpha_peak.
- Every 5 loops, generate age candidate. If improves, update.
- Every 10 loops, generate low candidate (weights low, sign +) to fill edges.
- If no improve for many, perturb.

Beta schedule for peak: Start beta = 0.5*n? But q range. We can adapt beta = `target_exp / max(qmax-qmin, 1/n^2)`. For q range ~1/n. Use `beta = 5.0 / max(qrange, 1e-300)` so exponent range 5. Then as q flattens, qrange smaller, beta increases? That might make weights always exponent range 5, focusing on current peaks. Good. For low weights, similarly. But if qrange tiny near optimum, beta huge; use cap to avoid underflow: `beta = min(1e7, 5/qrange)`. `qrange = q.max() - q.min()`. Since q max ~1e-4, beta~50000. Good.
For high weights active near exp5 spread. For more peaks, beta=1/qrange maybe. Use `peak_beta = min(5e6, 5/qrange)`.

Low weights: `w=softmax(-beta*q)` or ` softmax(beta*(qmin - q))` with beta similar. Exponent range 5.

Step update variants:
```
def mp_step_from_weights(p, weights, alpha, mode):
   t = cross_corr_p_w(p, weights)
   floor_t = 1e-300
   if mode == 'log_ratio':
      mean_t = np.dot(p, t)
      ratio = mean_t / np.maximum(t, floor_t)
      logchange = alpha * np.log(ratio)
      logchange = np.clip(logchange, -0.5, 0.5)
   elif mode == 'linear':
      mean_t = np.dot(p, t)
      rms = np.sqrt(np.mean((t - t.mean())**2)) + eps
      logchange = alpha * (mean_t - t) / (rms + eps) # if want decrease high? use sign
      logchange = np.clip(logchange, -0.5, 0.5)
   p_new = p * np.exp(logchange)
   normalize floor
```
For peak high weights, mode log_ratio (increase low t, decrease high t) maybe good. For low weights, maybe mode linear positive (increase where low q contributions high?) We can just try candidate and accept if score improves; if one mode not, next loops.

def softmax_p(x):
    x = x - np.max(x)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = e.sum()
    if s <= 0: return np.ones(len(x))/len(x)
    return e/s

def lse_obj_grad(x, beta):

def poly_inv(a, n):
   # a[0]!=0, find b length n with a*b=1 mod x^n
   b=[1/a[0]]
   m=1
   while m<n:
      m2=min(2m,n)

Pseudo:
```
def polish_sequence(p0, budget_s, rng, label=''):
    n = len(p0)
    p = normalize_p(np.clip(p0,0,None))
    best_p = p.copy(); best_score = score_p(p)
    alpha_peak = 0.1
    alpha_low = 0.05
    alpha_age = 0.05
    beta_factor = 5.0
    fails = 0
    t_start = time.monotonic(); t_end = t_start + budget_s
    it = 0
    q = conv_self(p); M=q.max(); score=2*n*M
    while time < t_end and n>0:
        it += 1
        # time check after each
        # Ensure q corresponds to p
        # generate candidates list
        # 1. Peak step
        qrange = max(M - q.min(), 1e-300)
        beta = min(1e7, 5.0/qrange)
        w = lse_weights(q, beta, low=False)
        p1 = power_step(p, w, alpha_peak, direction=-1)
        sc1 = score_p(p1)
        if sc1 < score - 1e-12:
            p=p1; score=sc1; update best; fails=0; alpha_peak=min(0.5, alpha_peak*1.03)
        else:
            # maybe try low
            wlow = lse_weights(q, beta, low=True)
            p2=power_step(p, wlow, alpha_low, direction=+1)
            sc2=score_p(p2)
            if sc2 < score - 1e-12:
                p=p2; score=sc2; update best; fails=0; alpha_low*=1.03; alpha_peak*=0.8
            else:
                fails +=1
                alpha_peak*=0.85; alpha_low*=0.85
                # maybe age every 10 if fails
        # AGE
        if it % 10 == 0 and n <= 20000:
            p3=age_step(p, q, alpha_age, n_active=40)
            if p3 is not None:
                sc3=score_p(p3)
                if sc3 < score - 1e-12:
                    p=p3; score=sc3; update best; fails=0; alpha_age*=1.1
                else: alpha_age*=0.8
        # sqrt reparam every 30
        if it % 30 == 0:
            p4=sqrtp_reparam(p, mix=0.5)
            sc4=score_p(p4)
            if sc4 < score - 1e-12:
                p=p4; score=sc4; update best; fails=0
            # maybe also mix1 if time? if sc4 not bad? skip
        # perturb if fails > 25
        if fails > 25:
            p = random_perturb_p(p, rng, mode=fails%3, scale=0.01 + 0.02*(fails/25))
            sc=score_p(p); update if better? maybe not; color
            fails = 10
            recompute q
        # update q for p each loop if p updated? We can recompute at top of loop. To avoid stale q after p updated within loop, set q=None and recompute at bottom? Simple: at end of loop recompute q=M-score for next.
        if time > t_end: break
        q=conv_self(p); M=q.max(); score=2*n*M
    # final small LSE L-BFGS if time and n reasonable
    rem = t_end - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 60 and n <= 20000:
        p_lb, sc_lb = run_lbfgs_lse(best_p, time_budget=rem*0.7, rng=None)
        if sc_lb < best_score: best_p=best_lb; best_score=sc_lb
    # final exact? maybe quick peak steps with best
    return best_p, best_score
```

`power_step` direction sign:
```
def power_step(p, weights, alpha, direction):
    t=cross_corr_p_w(p, weights)
    mean_t=float(np.dot(p,t))
    t_safe=np.maximum(t, mean_t*1e-30 + 1e-300)
    ratio=t_safe/mean_t
    log_change = direction * alpha * np.log(ratio)  # direction -1: high t -> negative
    # Actually if direction=-1 and ratio>1, log_change negative. Good.
    log_change=np.clip(log_change, -0.5, 0.5)
    p_new=p*np.exp(log_change)
    return normalize_p(p_new)
```
For direction +1 low weights: low lags weights make t high at indices contributing to low q; increases them. Good.

Maybe `mean_t` for high weights is LSE value ~M. For low weights, mean_t lower. Ratio centered.

def age_step(p, q, alpha, n_active=40):
    n=len(p); M=q.max()
    A=min(n_active, len(q))
    # top indices
    idx=np.argpartition(q, -A)[-A:] if A<len(q) else np.arange(len(q))
    idx=idx[np.argsort(q[idx])[::-1]]? not necessary for C columns order.
    C=np.zeros((n,A))
    for ai,m in enumerate(idx):
       m=int(m)
       start=max(0, m-n+1); end=min(m, n-1)
       if end>=start:
           ii=np.arange(start,end+1)
           C[ii,ai]=p[m-ii]
    target=np.ones(n)
    try:
       lam, res = nnls(C, target)
    except: lam=np.ones(A)/A
    s=lam.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300:
       lam=np.ones(A)/A
    else: lam=lam/s
    if np.all(lam <=0): fallback
    # Maybe if lam very concentrated? okay
    # t = cross_corr p with lam (weights length K? lam length A, need full weights vector)
    w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[idx]=lam

    t=cross_corr_p_w(p,w)
    mean_t=np.dot(p,t)
    if mean_t<=0: return None
    ratio=t/(mean_t+eps)
    logc = alpha * np.log(1.0/ratio) # direction -1
    clip, p_new normalize
    return p_new

def sqrtp_reparam(p, mix=1.0):
    q=conv_self(p)
    qsum=q.sum()
    if qsum<=0: return p
    q=q/qsum + 1e-15 # maybe add floor

    rp=np.maximum(r,0)
    rp_sum=rp.sum()
    if rp_sum <= 1e-12: return p
    rp/=rp_sum
    if mix>=1: return rp
    return normalize_p((1-mix)*p + mix*rp)

def causal_sqrt_seed(n, mode='uniform'):
    K=2*n-1; m=np.arange(K, dtype=float)
    if mode=='uniform': w=np.ones(K)
    elif mode=='decay_right': w=np.exp(-0.7*m/K)
    elif mode=='decay_left': w=np.exp(-0.7*((K-1-m)/K))
    elif mode=='center_dip':
       mid=(K-1)/2; w=1 - 0.25*np.exp(-((m-mid)/(0.2*K))**2)
       w=np.maximum(w,1e-3)
    elif mode=='center_peak':
       w=1 + 0.5*np.exp(-((m-mid)/(0.3*K))**2)
    w/=w.sum()
    # compute r up to maybe n+offset? offset_min = int(0.15*n)

    r=causal_sqrt_seq(w, L)
    r_pos=np.maximum(r,0)
    if mode<'? offset': choose segment start = int(offset_frac*(L-n))
    seg=r_pos[start:start+n]
    if sum<=0 uniform
    # optionally mix with reversed seg
    return normalize(seg)

```
def lse_obj_grad(x,beta):
  p=softmax_p(x)
  n=len(p); q=conv_self(p); M=q.max()
  scaled=beta*(q-M)
  # scaled max 0; but due clip maybe
  scaled=np.clip(scaled, -745, 709)
  exps=np.exp(scaled)
  sumexp=exps.sum()
  if sumexp<=0: exps[int(np.argmax(q))]=1; sumexp=1
  w=exps/sumexp
  L=M + np.log(sumexp)/beta
  score=2*n*L
  t=cross_corr_p_w(p,w)
  grad_p=2*t
  grad_x=p*(grad_p - np.dot(p, grad_p))
  return score, grad_x
```

### `normalize_p`
```
def normalize_p(p, floor=1e-18):
    p=np.asarray(p, dtype=float)
    p=np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p=np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    S=p.sum()
    if S <= 0:
       return np.ones(len(p), dtype=float)/max(1,len(p))
    p=p/S
    # if very spiky >1000? p <=1, fine.
    # apply floor
    p=np.maximum(p, floor)
    S=p.sum(); if S<=0: uniform
    return p/S
```
Applying floor after normalization increases sum tiny; okay. But if n huge 1e5, floor n*1e-18=1e-13 negligible. Use floor=1e-20? float min. Let's use `floor=1e-18`.

#### `next_pow2`
```
def next_pow2(m):
    m=int(max(1,m))
    return 1 << (m-1).bit_length()
```

#### `normalize_p`

#### `conv_self`
```
def conv_self(a):
    n=len(a); K=2*n-1
    L=next_pow2(K)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(A*A, L)[:K]
    # remove tiny negatives
    c = c.real
    # For speed, do not max? But negative small can affect score? max max. Use maximum(c,0)
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)
```
For q sum due to normalization maybe 1+error. `np.maximum` may slightly change sum. Fine.

#### `cross_corr_p_w`
```
def cross_corr_p_w(p, w):
    n=len(p); K=2*n-1
    L=next_pow2(3*n-2)
    r=np.asarray(w, dtype=float)[::-1]
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    R=np.fft.rfft(r, L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(P*R, L)
    idx = 2*n-2 - np.arange(n)
    return c[idx].real
```
If w is sparse, still full.

#### `score_p`
```
def score_p(p):
    n=len(p); q=conv_self(p); mq=float(np.max(q)); return 2.0*n*mq
```

#### `lse_weights`
```
def lse_weights(q, beta, low=False):
    K=len(q); qmax=q.max()
    if low:
       scaled = beta * (qmax - q)  # low q => high exponent? Actually if low q, qmax-q positive; we want high weight for low q, yes.
    else:
       scaled = beta * (q - qmax)  # active peak high (max 0)
    scaled=np.clip(scaled, -745.0, 745.0)
    e=np.exp(scaled)
    s=e.sum()
    if s <= 0:
       w=np.zeros(K); w[int(np.argmax(q if not low else -q))]=1.0; return w
    return e/s
```
For low weights with beta=5/qrange, scaled range 0..5, sum exp ok. For high weights scaled <=0. If beta huge, many underflow to 0 but max exps 1; sum maybe number of peaks; okay. If all underflow except max? sum>=1.

#### `power_step`
```
def power_step(p, weights, alpha, direction):
    t = cross_corr_p_w(p, weights)
    t = np.maximum(t, 0.0)
    mean_t = float(np.dot(p, t))
    if mean_t <= 0:
       return p.copy()
    t_safe = np.maximum(t, mean_t * 1e-30)
    ratio = t_safe / mean_t
    logc = direction * float(alpha) * np.log(ratio)
    logc = np.clip(logc, -0.35, 0.35)
    p_new = p * np.exp(logc)
    return normalize_p(p_new)
```
For direction=-1 high t reduces. For direction=+1 low weights increases high t (contributing low q). Good.

#### `age_step`
Need `nnls` may produce lam large; C target ones maybe. We can target `C` rows? Let's use target = ones(n) not M.
```
def age_step(p, q, alpha, n_active=50):
    n=len(p); K=2*n-1
    A = int(min(n_active, K, max(20, n//200)))? If n small, A maybe >K.
    if A >= K: idx=np.arange(K)
    else:
       # use argpartition for top A
       part = np.argpartition(q, -A)
       idx = part[-A:]
    # sort not needed
    C = np.zeros((n, A), dtype=float)
    for ai, mv in enumerate(idx):
       m=int(mv)
       start=max(0, m-n+1); end=min(m,n-1)
       if end >= start:
          ii=np.arange(start,end+1)
          C[ii, ai] = p[m - ii]
    if not np.any(C): return None
    target = np.ones(n)
    try:
       lam, res = nnls(C, target)
    except Exception:
       lam = np.ones(A, dtype=float)
    s=float(lam.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
       lam = np.ones(A, dtype=float); s=A
    elif not np.all(np.isfinite(lam)):
       lam=np.maximum(np.where(np.isfinite(lam), lam, 0.0),0.0)
       s=lam.sum()
       if s<=1e-300: lam=np.ones(A); s=A
    lam=lam/s
    # direct sparse t
    t=np.zeros(n)
    for ai, mv in enumerate(idx):
       l=lam[ai]
       if l <= 0: continue
       m=int(mv)
       start=max(0,m-n+1); end=min(m,n-1)
       if end>=start:
          ii=np.arange(start,end+1)
          t[ii] += l * p[m-ii]
    mean_t=float(np.dot(p,t))
    if mean_t <= 0: return None
    t_safe=np.maximum(t, mean_t*1e-30)
    logc = -float(alpha) * np.log(t_safe/mean_t)  # reduce high t
    logc=np.clip(logc, -0.25,0.25)
    return normalize_p(p*np.exp(logc))
```
If age active lags top all with equal t? OK. `n_active` choose 60.

Potential issue: `np.argpartition(q, -A)` if A=0? A>=1.

#### `causal_sqrt_seq`
```
def causal_sqrt_seq(u, n):
    u=np.asarray(u, dtype=float)
    r=np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    r0=float(u[0]) if n>0 else 0.0
    if r0 <= 1e-300:
       r0=1e-15
    r[0]=np.sqrt(r0)
    inv2r0=0.5 / r[0]
    for k in range(1,n):
       if k < len(u): uk=float(u[k])
       else: uk=0.0
       if k >= 2:
           # r[1:k] length k-1, reversed r[k-1:0:-1]
           cross = float(np.dot(r[1:k], r[k-1:0:-1]))
       else:
           cross = 0.0
       val = (uk - cross) * inv2r0
       # If val becomes huge due to negative? clip?
       if not np.isfinite(val): val = 0.0
       # To avoid runaway, cap maybe 1e6
       if val > 1e6: val=1e6
       if val < -1e6: val=-1e6
       r[k]=val
    return r
```
For positive uniform first n val positive. Cap not affect likely. The recurrence cross uses r values that may be negative if later; okay.

But dot `r[1:k]` and `r[k-1:0:-1]` when k=2: r[1:2] length1, r[1:0:-1] -> r[1] length1, cross=r1^2. Good.

Performance: For each k, slicing and dot. For n=10000, okay. Could optimize by using `np.dot(r[1:k], r[:k-1][::-1])` similar.

#### `causal_sqrt_seed`
Simplify modes.
```
def causal_sqrt_seed(n, mode='uniform'):
    K=2*n-1
    m=np.arange(K, dtype=float)
    if mode == 'uniform': w=np.ones(K)
    elif mode == 'decay_right': w=np.exp(-0.8*m/K)
    elif mode == 'decay_left': w=np.exp(-0.8*(K-1-m)/K)
    elif mode == 'center_dip':
       mid=(K-1)/2.0; w=1.0 - 0.25*np.exp(-((m-mid)/(0.25*K))**2); w=np.maximum(w, 0.1)
    elif mode == 'center_peak':
       mid=(K-1)/2.0; w=1.0 + 0.5*np.exp(-((m-mid)/(0.35*K))**2)
    else: w=np.ones(K)
    s=w.sum(); w=w/s
    L=int(min(2*n, int(1.25*n)+1))
    # target length at least L; if L>K, u padded zeros? K=2n-1 >1.25n for n>1, so L<K, ok
    r=causal_sqrt_seq(w, L)
    rpos=np.maximum(r, 0.0)
    # choose offset based on mode? For uniform, try offset 0, 0.25, 0.5 by mode param? We can split modes names.
```
Need include offset. Let's make `causal_sqrt_seed(n, target='uniform', offset_frac=0.0, mix=1.0)`.
```
    L=int(min(2*n, int(1.25*n)+1))
    r=causal_sqrt_seq(w,L)
    rpos=np.maximum(r,0)
    total_span=L-n
    if total_span>0 and offset_frac>0:
       start=int(offset_frac*total_span)
    else: start=0
    seg=rpos[start:start+n]
    if seg.sum() <=0: seg=rpos[:n]
    if seg.sum() <=0: return uniform
    p=normalize_p(seg)
    if mix is not None and 0<mix<1:
       p=normalize_p((1-mix)*p + mix*p[::-1])
    return p
```
If L<n? no.

Modes list for seeds:
- ('uniform',0,1)
- ('uniform',0.3,1)
- ('decay_right',0,1)
- ('center_dip',0,1)
- maybe mix: normalize 0.6*cause +0.4*reverse.
Evaluate and pick.

#### `sqrtp_reparam`
```
def sqrtp_reparam(p, mix=1.0):
    n=len(p)
    q=conv_self(p)
    s=q.sum()
    if s<=0: return p.copy()
    q=q/s
    q=q + 1e-15/q.size  # floor
    r=causal_sqrt_seq(q, n)
    rp=np.maximum(r,0)
    sumr=rp.sum()
    if sumr<=1e-12:
       return p.copy()
    rp/=sumr
    if mix >=1: return rp
    return normalize_p((1-mix)*p + mix*rp)
```
If p sparse, q0 may small; floor.

#### `random_perturb_p`
```
def random_perturb_p(p, rng, mode=0, scale=0.02):
   n=len(p)
   if mode==0:
      pos=np.arange(n)/max(1,n)
      f=int(rng.choice([1,2,3,4,6,8,12]))
      phase=float(2*np.pi*rng.random())
      mod=1.0 + scale*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*pos + phase)
      p_new=p*mod
   elif mode==1:
      # random high/low transfer
      p_new=p.copy()
      q=np.quantile(p, 0.75)
      high=p_new > q
      if high.sum()==0 or high.sum()==n: return normalize_p(p)
      low=~high
      transfer=0.05 * float(p_new[high].sum()) / max(1, high.sum())
      p_new[high] -= transfer
      p_new[low] += transfer * high.sum() / max(1, low.sum())
   else:
      p_new=p * (1.0 + scale*rng.standard_normal(n))
   return normalize_p(p_new)
```
`np.quantile` for n large okay. Could use `np.sort`? fine.

#### `resize_p`
```
def resize_p(p, n_new):
    n=len(p)
    if n==n_new: return p.copy()
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_new)
    q=np.interp(x_new, x_old, np.clip(p,0,None), left=0.0, right=0.0)
    return normalize_p(q)
```

#### `softmax_p`
```
def softmax_p(x):
    x=x - np.max(x)
    e=np.exp(x)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0:
       return np.ones(len(x))/len(x)
    return e/s
```

#### `lse_obj_grad`
Need `cross_corr_p_w` with weights sum1. Use `np.dot(p, grad_p)`.
For beta maybe 0? If beta<=0, uniform weights. Set beta=max(beta,1e-12).
```
def lse_obj_grad(x,beta):
   p=softmax_p(x)
   n=len(p); q=conv_self(p); M=q.max()
   if beta <= 0:
      w=np.ones(2*n-1)/(2*n-1); L=M
   else:
      scaled=beta*(q-M)
      scaled=np.clip(scaled, -745, 745)
      e=np.exp(scaled)
      s=e.sum()
      if s<=0: w=np.zeros(K); w[argmax]=1
      else: w=e/s
      L=M + np.log(s)/beta
   score=2*n*L
   t=cross_corr_p_w(p,w)
   grad_p=2.0*t
   gdot=float(np.dot(p,grad_p))
   grad_x=p*(grad_p - gdot)
   return score, grad_x
```
Check L when beta large and s sum exp scaled where max scaled 0. If all e=0? max at q=M gives exp(0)=1, so s≥1. Good.

#### `run_lbfgs_lse`
```
def run_lbfgs_lse(p, time_budget):
   t0=time.monotonic(); t_end=t0+max(1.0,time_budget)
   n=len(p)
   p=np.array(p,float); best_p=p; best_score=score_p(p)
   x=np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-15/n)); x-=np.mean(x)
   # betas scale with n
   betas=[1.0, 10.0, 100.0] # dimensionless? Need absolute: beta = factor / (q range). q range ~1/n. So absolute ~ factor*n.
   q=conv_self(best_p); qrange=max(q.max()-q.min(),1e-12)
   base_beta=5.0/qrange
   for factor in [1.0,3.0,10.0,30.0]:
      beta=base_beta*factor
      if time.monotonic() > t_end - 30: break
      maxfun=300
      try:
         res=minimize(lse_obj_grad, x, args=(beta,), method='L-BFGS-B', jac=True, options={'maxfun':maxfun,'maxiter':200,'ftol':1e-15,'gtol':1e-12,'maxls':30})
      except Exception:
         break
      try: p_new=softmax_p(res.x)
      except: break
      p_new=normalize_p(p_new)
      sc=score_p(p_new)
      if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
         best_score=sc; best_p=p_new
         x=np.log(np.maximum(best_p,1e-15/n)); x-=np.mean(x)
      if time.monotonic() > t_end - 20: break
   return best_p, best_score
```
If tuple not supported, exception, break. Could fallback to multiplicative only. Fine.

`base_beta` from current qrange. If qrange small near optimum, base_beta large; factor 30 may huge; clipping handles. Maybe cap beta at 1e8.

#### `polish_sequence`
Need pass `verbose_label`? We'll have it print.
```
def polish_sequence(p0, budget_s, rng, label=''):
    n=len(p0)
    p=normalize_p(np.clip(p0,0,None))
    best=p.copy(); best_score=score_p(p)
    if n==0 or best_score==np.inf: return best,best_score
    alpha_peak=0.10; alpha_low=0.05; alpha_age=0.05
    fails=0; age_done=0; sqrt_done=0
    t_start=time.monotonic(); t_end=t_start+budget_s
    q=conv_self(p); M=q.max(); score=2*n*M
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
       it+=1
       if it % 200 ==0: print(...)
       # peak
       qrange=max(M - float(q.min()), 1e-300)
       beta=min(1e8, 5.0/qrange)
       w=lse_weights(q,beta,low=False)
       p1=power_step(p,w,alpha_peak,-1)
       sc1=score_p(p1)
       if sc1 < score - 1e-12:
          p=p1; score=sc1; fails=0; alpha_peak=min(0.35, alpha_peak*1.03)
          if score < best_score - 1e-12: best_score=score; best=p.copy(); print?
       else:
          # low
          wlow=lse_weights(q,beta,low=True)
          p2=power_step(p,wlow,alpha_low,+1)
          sc2=score_p(p2)
          if sc2 < score - 1e-12:
             p=p2; score=sc2; fails=0; alpha_low=min(0.25, alpha_low*1.05)
             update best
          else:
             fails +=1
             alpha_peak=max(0.01, alpha_peak*0.8)
             alpha_low=max(0.01, alpha_low*0.8)
       # age every 8 if age_done<25
       if it % 8 == 0 and age_done < 25 and n <= 20000:
          age_done +=1
          p3=age_step(p,q,alpha_age,n_active=60)
          if p3 is not None:
             sc3=score_p(p3)
             if sc3 < score - 1e-12:
                p=p3; score=sc3; fails=0; alpha_age=min(0.25, alpha_age*1.1)
                update best
             else:
                alpha_age=max(0.01, alpha_age*0.8)
          # recompute q
          q=conv_self(p); M=q.max(); score=2*n*M
       # sqrt reparam every 30 if sqrt_done<8
       if it % 30 == 0 and sqrt_done < 8 and n <= 20000:
          sqrt_done +=1
          p4=sqrtp_reparam(p, 0.5)
          sc4=score_p(p4)
          if sc4 < score - 1e-12:
             p=p4; score=sc4; fails=0
             update best
          # even if not improve, maybe keep? no
          # recompute q
          q=conv_self(p); M=q.max(); score=2*n*M
       # perturb if fails large
       if fails > 30:
          p=random_perturb_p(p, rng, mode=(fails%3), scale=0.01 + 0.03*min(1.0, fails/50.0))
          sc=score_p(p)
          if sc < score - 1e-12:
             score=sc
          elif sc < best_score:
             score=sc
          fails=10
          q=conv_self(p); M=q.max(); score=2*n*M
       # update q at bottom if not updated? For peak accepted p changed but q stale; recompute every loop.
       if it % 2 ==0 or p changed? We need recompute q regardless each loop after any accept. Simpler: at bottom:
       q=conv_self(p); M=float(q.max()); score=2*n*M
       if score < best_score - 1e-12: best_score=score; best=p.copy(); print maybe
    # final L-BFGS small if time
    rem=t_end-time.monotonic()
    if rem > 60 and n <= 20000:
       p_lb, sc_lb = run_lbfgs_lse(best, rem*0.8)
       if sc_lb < best_score:
          best_score=sc_lb; best=p_lb
    return best, best_score
```
This recomputes q every loop (extra conv). But needed for next. We can avoid some by only recompute at bottom and use it for next; the `q` used for age/sqrt is from beginning loop; after accept inside loop, age uses stale q if p changed before age in same loop? Actually we do peak first, then age in same loop if fail/accept. If peak accepted, q stale. Should recompute before age. Simplify structure: At top, q current. For each method candidate, if accept p new, break to next loop (recompute q). This reduces stale but more loops.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-height sequences minimizing

    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a))^2

using a much wider toolkit than the previous LSE/subgradient code:

- parent candidate, if available, is the primary seed
- polynomial causal square-root seeds (exact/low-lag factors of a target
  convolution shape)
- multiplicative log-space moves driven by LSE/active-set weights
- active-set NNLS subgradient equalization (KKT-like polishing)
- occasional "square-root reparametrization" p -> sqrt(p*p)
- limited LSE softmax L-BFGS refinement
- resize / restart exploration and final oracle verification

The returned list is scaled to have sum 1, which is harmless because the
score is scale-invariant.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from scipy.optimize import minimize, nnls


def next_pow2(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()


def normalize_p(p, floor=1e-18):
    a = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    a = a / s
    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
    s2 = float(a.sum())
    if s2 <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)
    return a / s2


def make_uniform_p(n):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n)


def conv_self(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    K = 2 * n - 1
    L = next_pow2(K)
    A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(A * A, L)[:K]
    c = c.real
    # The exact convolution is non-negative. Numerical noise can create tiny
    # negative values; clipping is harmless and stabilizes log/weight choices.
    return np.maximum(c, 0.0)


def score_p(p):
    a = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.inf
    q = conv_self(a / s)
    mq = float(np.max(q))
    return 2.0 * float(n) * mq


def cross_corr_p_w(p, w):
    """
    Returns t_i = sum_j p_j w_{i+j}, i = 0..n-1.
    This is (up to a factor of 2) the convolution-weight subgradient used in
    the LSE/active-set multipliers.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    K = 2 * n - 1
    if w.size != K:
        if w.size < K:
            tmp = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
            tmp[:min(K, w.size)] = w
            w = tmp
        else:
            w = w[:K]
    L = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    r = w[::-1]
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    R = np.fft.rfft(r, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(P * R, L)
    idx = 2 * n - 2 - np.arange(n)
    return c[idx].real


def lse_weights(q, beta, low=False):
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    K = q.size
    if K == 0:
        return q
    qmax = float(np.max(q))
    if low:
        scaled = beta * (qmax - q)
    else:
        scaled = beta * (q - qmax)
    scaled = np.clip(scaled, -745.0, 745.0)
    e = np.exp(scaled)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        w = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
        if low:
            k = int(np.argmin(q))
        else:
            k = int(np.argmax(q))
        w[k] = 1.0
        return w
    return e / s


def power_step(p, weights, alpha, direction):
    """
    Multiplicative log-space update.
    direction = -1: reduce coordinates with large weighted gradient (peak shaving)
    direction = +1: increase coordinates with large weighted gradient (filling low lags)
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if p.size == 0:
        return p.copy()
    t = cross_corr_p_w(p, weights)
    t = np.maximum(t, 0.0)
    mean_t = float(np.dot(p, t))
    if mean_t <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()
    t_safe = np.maximum(t, mean_t * 1e-30)
    ratio = t_safe / mean_t
    logc = float(direction) * float(alpha) * np.log(ratio)
    logc = np.clip(logc, -0.35, 0.35)
    p_new = p * np.exp(logc)
    return normalize_p(p_new)


def age_step(p, q, alpha, n_active=60):
    """
    Active-set KKT-like step.
    Solves a small NNLS for weights on the currently largest convolution lags
    that try to make the weighted subgradient as flat as possible, then moves
    multiplicatively against it.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    q = np.asarray(q, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    K = q.size
    if n == 0 or K == 0:
        return None

    A = int(min(max(1, n_active), K, max(10, n // 200)))
    if A >= K:
        idx = np.arange(K)
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(q, -A)[-A:]
        idx = idx[np.argsort(q[idx])[::-1]]

    C = np.zeros((n, A), dtype=np.float64)
    for ai, mv in enumerate(idx):
        m = int(mv)
        start = max(0, m - n + 1)
        end = min(m, n - 1)
        if end >= start:
            ii = np.arange(start, end + 1)
            C[ii, ai] = p[m - ii]

    if not np.any(C):
        return None

    target = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    try:
        lam, _ = nnls(C, target)
    except Exception:
        lam = np.ones(A, dtype=np.float64)

    lam = np.where(np.isfinite(lam), lam, 0.0)
    lam = np.maximum(lam, 0.0)
    s = float(lam.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        lam = np.ones(A, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(A)
    lam = lam / s

    t = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    for ai, mv in enumerate(idx):
        l = float(lam[ai])
        if l <= 0.0:
            continue
        m = int(mv)
        start = max(0, m - n + 1)
        end = min(m, n - 1)
        if end >= start:
            ii = np.arange(start, end + 1)
            t[ii] += l * p[m - ii]

    mean_t = float(np.dot(p, t))
    if mean_t <= 1e-300:
        return None

    t_safe = np.maximum(t, mean_t * 1e-30)
    ratio = t_safe / mean_t
    logc = -float(alpha) * np.log(ratio)
    logc = np.clip(logc, -0.25, 0.25)
    return normalize_p(p * np.exp(logc))


def causal_sqrt_seq(u, n):
    """
    Formal causal square root of a power series u:
        R(z)^2 = u(z),  R(z) = r_0 + r_1 z + ...
    Returns r[0:n]. Used to build seeds and to reparametrize current q=p*p.
    """
    u = np.asarray(u, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    r = np.zeros(int(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if r.size == 0:
        return r
    r0 = float(u[0]) if u.size > 0 else 0.0
    if r0 <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(r0):
        r0 = 1e-15
    r[0] = np.sqrt(r0)
    inv2r0 = 0.5 / r[0]

    for k in range(1, r.size):
        if k < u.size:
            uk = float(u[k])
        else:
            uk = 0.0
        if k >= 2:
            cross = float(np.dot(r[1:k], r[k - 1:0:-1]))
        else:
            cross = 0.0
        val = (uk - cross) * inv2r0
        if not np.isfinite(val):
            val = 0.0
        if val > 1e6:
            val = 1e6
        elif val < -1e6:
            val = -1e6
        r[k] = val
    return r


def causal_sqrt_seed(n, target="uniform", offset_frac=0.0, mix=1.0):
    """
    Build a non-negative seed by taking the causal square root of a chosen
    target convolution shape.
    """
    n = int(max(2, n))
    K = 2 * n - 1
    m = np.arange(K, dtype=np.float64)

    if target == "decay_right":
        w = np.exp(-0.8 * m / K)
    elif target == "decay_left":
        w = np.exp(-0.8 * (K - 1 - m) / K)
    elif target == "center_dip":
        mid = 0.5 * (K - 1)
        w = 1.0 - 0.25 * np.exp(-((m - mid) / (0.25 * K)) ** 2)
        w = np.maximum(w, 0.05)
    elif target == "center_peak":
        mid = 0.5 * (K - 1)
        w = 1.0 + 0.5 * np.exp(-((m - mid) / (0.35 * K)) ** 2)
    else:
        w = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64)

    w = np.maximum(w, 0.0)
    sw = float(w.sum())
    if sw <= 0:
        w = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64)
        sw = float(K)
    w = w / sw

    # Compute a modest tail around the main window so offsets are possible.
    L = int(min(2 * n, int(1.25 * n) + 1))
    L = max(n, L)
    r = causal_sqrt_seq(w, L)
    r = np.maximum(r, 0.0)

    span = L - n
    if span > 0 and offset_frac > 0.0:
        start = int(offset_frac * span)
    else:
        start = 0
    start = max(0, min(start, L - n))
    seg = r[start:start + n]
    if seg.size < n:
        seg = np.pad(seg, (0, n - seg.size), mode="edge")

    ss = float(seg.sum())
    if ss <= 1e-12:
        p = np.maximum(r[:n], 0.0)
        ss = float(p.sum())
        if ss <= 1e-12:
            return make_uniform_p(n)
        seg = p / ss

    p = normalize_p(seg)
    if mix is not None and 0.0 < float(mix) < 1.0:
        p = normalize_p((1.0 - float(mix)) * p + float(mix) * p[::-1])
    return p


def square_root_reparam(p, mix=1.0):
    """
    Given current p and its convolution q, replace p by the causal square root
    of q (mixed with p for stability). This enforces self-consistency between
    p and p*p.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p.copy()
    q = conv_self(p)
    s = float(q.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return p.copy()
    q = q / s
    q = q + 1e-15 / float(max(1, q.size))
    r = causal_sqrt_seq(q, n)
    r = np.maximum(r, 0.0)
    ss = float(r.sum())
    if ss <= 1e-12:
        return p.copy()
    r = r / ss
    if mix is None or float(mix) >= 1.0:
        return normalize_p(r)
    return normalize_p((1.0 - float(mix)) * p + float(mix) * r)


def random_perturb_p(p, rng, mode=0, scale=0.02):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p.copy()

    if mode == 0:
        pos = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n - 1))
        f = int(rng.choice([1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12]))
        phase = float(2.0 * np.pi * rng.random())
        mod = 1.0 + float(scale) * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f * pos + phase)
        p_new = p * mod
    elif mode == 1:
        p_new = p.copy()
        q75 = float(np.quantile(p, 0.75))
        high = p_new > q75
        if high.sum() == 0 or high.sum() == n:
            return normalize_p(p)
        low = ~high
        transfer = 0.05 * float(p_new[high].sum()) / float(max(1, high.sum()))
        p_new[high] -= transfer
        p_new[low] += transfer * float(high.sum()) / float(max(1, low.sum()))
    else:
        p_new = p * (1.0 + float(scale) * rng.standard_normal(n))

    return normalize_p(np.maximum(p_new, 0.0))


def resize_p(p, n_new):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    n_new = int(max(1, n_new))
    if n == n_new:
        return p.copy()
    if n == 0:
        return make_uniform_p(n_new)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_new)
    q = np.interp(x_new, x_old, np.maximum(p, 0.0), left=0.0, right=0.0)
    return normalize_p(q)


def softmax_p(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    x = x - np.max(x)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return make_uniform_p(x.size)
    return e / s


def lse_obj_grad(x, beta):
    p = softmax_p(x)
    n = p.size
    q = conv_self(p)
    K = q.size
    M = float(np.max(q))

    if beta <= 1e-12:
        w = np.ones(K, dtype=np.float64) / float(K)
        L = M
    else:
        scaled = beta * (q - M)
        scaled = np.clip(scaled, -745.0, 745.0)
        e = np.exp(scaled)
        s = float(e.sum())
        if s <= 1e-300:
            w = np.zeros(K, dtype=np.float64)
            w[int(np.argmax(q))] = 1.0
            L = M
        else:
            w = e / s
            L = M + np.log(s) / beta

    score = 2.0 * float(n) * L
    t = cross_corr_p_w(p, w)
    grad_p = 2.0 * t
    gdot = float(np.dot(p, grad_p))
    grad_x = p * (grad_p - gdot)
    return score, grad_x


def run_lbfgs_lse(p, time_budget):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    if n == 0 or time_budget <= 5:
        return p, score_p(p)

    t0 = time.monotonic()
    t_end = t0 + max(1.0, float(time_budget))
    best_p = normalize_p(p)
    best_score = score_p(best_p)

    x = np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-15 / float(n)))
    x = x - float(np.mean(x))

    q = conv_self(best_p)
    qrange = max(float(np.max(q) - np.min(q)), 1e-12)
    base_beta = min(1e8, 5.0 / qrange)

    maxfun = 180 if n < 10000 else 90

    factors = [1.0, 3.0, 10.0, 30.0]
    for factor in factors:
        if time.monotonic() > t_end - 30.0:
            break
        beta = base_beta * float(factor)
        try:
            res = minimize(
                lse_obj_grad,
                x,
                args=(beta,),
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                jac=True,
                options={
                    "maxfun": int(maxfun),
                    "maxiter": 200,
                    "ftol": 1e-15,
                    "gtol": 1e-12,
                    "maxls": 30,
                },
            )
        except Exception:
            break

        try:
            p_new = softmax_p(res.x)
        except Exception:
            break
        p_new = normalize_p(p_new)
        sc = score_p(p_new)
        if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc
            best_p = p_new
            x = np.log(np.maximum(best_p, 1e-15 / float(n)))
            x = x - float(np.mean(x))

    return best_p, best_score


def polish_sequence(p0, budget_s, rng, label=""):
    p = normalize_p(np.asarray(p0, dtype=np.float64).ravel())
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return p, np.inf

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = score_p(best_p)
    if not np.isfinite(best_score):
        return best_p, best_score

    alpha_peak = 0.10
    alpha_low = 0.05
    alpha_age = 0.05
    fails = 0
    age_done = 0
    sqrt_done = 0
    iters = 0

    t_start = time.monotonic()
    t_end = t_start + max(20.0, float(budget_s))

    while time.monotonic() < t_end:
        p = normalize_p(p)
        q = conv_self(p)
        M = float(np.max(q))
        score = 2.0 * float(n) * M

        if score < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = score
            best_p = p.copy()
            if iters % 700 == 0 or best_score < 1.5035:
                print(f"  [polish:{label}] iter={iters} score={best_score:.8f}")

        improved = False

        qrange = max(M - float(np.min(q)), 1e-300)
        beta = min(1e8, 5.0 / qrange)

        # 1) Peak-shaving multiplicative step (LSE weights, decrease high t)
        w = lse_weights(q, beta, low=False)
        p1 = power_step(p, w, alpha_peak, -1)
        sc1 = score_p(p1)
        if sc1 < score - 1e-12:
            p = p1
            alpha_peak = min(0.35, alpha_peak * 1.04)
            fails = 0
            improved = True
        else:
            alpha_peak = max(0.01, alpha_peak * 0.82)

            # 2) Low-lag filling multiplicative step
            wl = lse_weights(q, beta, low=True)
            p2 = power_step(p, wl, alpha_low, +1)
            sc2 = score_p(p2)
            if sc2 < score - 1e-12:
                p = p2
                alpha_low = min(0.28, alpha_low * 1.06)
                fails = 0
                improved = True
            else:
                alpha_low = max(0.01, alpha_low * 0.82)

                # 3) Active-set NNLS equalization, occasionally
                if (iters % 8 == 0) and age_done < 28 and n <= 22000:
                    age_done += 1
                    p3 = age_step(p, q, alpha_age, n_active=60)
                    if p3 is not None:
                        sc3 = score_p(p3)
                        if sc3 < score - 1e-12:
                            p = p3
                            alpha_age = min(0.25, alpha_age * 1.12)
                            fails = 0
                            improved = True
                        else:
                            alpha_age = max(0.01, alpha_age * 0.78)

                # 4) Polynomial square-root reparametrization, rarely
                if (not improved) and (iters % 34 == 0) and sqrt_done < 8 and n <= 18000:
                    sqrt_done += 1
                    p4 = square_root_reparam(p, 0.45)
                    sc4 = score_p(p4)
                    if sc4 < score - 1e-12:
                        p = p4
                        fails = 0
                        improved = True

                # 5) Random smoothing restart if stuck
                if (not improved) and fails > 28:
                    p = random_perturb_p(p, rng, mode=(fails % 3),
                                         scale=0.012 + 0.025 * min(1.0, fails / 60.0))
                    sc5 = score_p(p)
                    if sc5 < score - 1e-12:
                        score = sc5
                    if fails % 60 == 0:
                        alpha_peak = max(alpha_peak, 0.08)
                        alpha_low = max(alpha_low, 0.04)
                        alpha_age = max(alpha_age, 0.04)
                    fails = 8
                    improved = False

        iters += 1

        # Deliberately recompute q at the top of next loop via p above.
        # This structure uses exactly one fresh q per full loop when a step
        # is accepted, and a few candidate scores when it is not.

    rem = t_end - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 70.0 and n <= 18000:
        p_lb, sc_lb = run_lbfgs_lse(best_p, rem * 0.85)
        if sc_lb < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = sc_lb
            best_p = p_lb
            print(f"  [polish:{label} L-BFGS] score={best_score:.8f}")

    return best_p, best_score


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400):
    """
    Returns the best non-negative coefficient list found within budget_s seconds.
    """
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    total_budget = max(60.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t_start + total_budget - 20.0
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))

    MAX_OPT_N = 16500

    # ----------------------- load parent if available -----------------------
    parent = None
    try:
        hv = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
        if hv is not None:
            arr = np.asarray(hv, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if arr.size > 0:
                arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
                arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
                if float(arr.sum()) > 1e-12:
                    parent = normalize_p(arr)
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None:
        if parent.size > MAX_OPT_N:
            parent_opt = resize_p(parent, MAX_OPT_N)
        else:
            parent_opt = parent.copy()
        n_base = parent_opt.size
    else:
        parent_opt = None
        n_base = 8192

    # Optional cap for very large parents: still keep parent for final oracle
    # comparison, but optimize a resized copy.
    if parent is not None and parent.size > MAX_OPT_N:
        pass

    candidates = []

    if parent_opt is not None:
        candidates.append((parent_opt.copy(), score_p(parent_opt), "parent"))

    # Uniform is a very poor seed (score 2), but useful only if no parent exists
    # and seeds fail.
    if parent is None:
        candidates.append((make_uniform_p(n_base), 2.0, "uniform"))

    # ----------------------- polynomial causal-sqrt seeds -----------------------
    seed_deadline = t_start + min(180.0, 0.12 * total_budget)
    seed_params = [
        ("uniform", 0.0, 1.0),
        ("uniform", 0.25, 1.0),
        ("decay_right", 0.0, 1.0),
        ("center_dip", 0.0, 1.0),
    ]

    max_seeds = 4
    if n_base > 12000:
        max_seeds = 2
    if n_base > 15000:
        max_seeds = 1

    for target, off, mix in seed_params[:max_seeds]:
        if time.monotonic() > seed_deadline:
            break
        if n_base > 18000:
            break
        try:
            ps = causal_sqrt_seed(n_base, target=target, offset_frac=off, mix=mix)
            ss = score_p(ps)
            candidates.append((ps, ss, f"poly_{target}_{off:.2f}"))
        except Exception:
            pass

    # If no candidates (very small budget etc.), fallback.
    if not candidates:
        n0 = max(64, int(min(MAX_OPT_N, n_base)))
        return [float(x) for x in make_uniform_p(n0)]

    # Sort candidates by internal score without lambda functions.
    remaining = list(candidates)
    sorted_candidates = []
    while remaining:
        bi = 0
        bv = remaining[0][1]
        for i in range(1, len(remaining)):
            if remaining[i][1] < bv:
                bv = remaining[i][1]
                bi = i
        sorted_candidates.append(remaining.pop(bi))

    if len(sorted_candidates) > 4:
        sorted_candidates = sorted_candidates[:4]

    global_best_p = sorted_candidates[0][0].copy()
    global_best_score = sorted_candidates[0][1]
    print(f"Initial best: {global_best_score:.8f}, n={global_best_p.size}, label={sorted_candidates[0][2]}")
    for p, sc, lab in sorted_candidates:
        print(f"   candidate {lab}: internal={sc:.8f}, n={p.size}")

    # ----------------------- main polishing phases -----------------------
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    first_budget = max(60.0, 0.68 * rem)

    if len(sorted_candidates) == 1:
        fracs = [1.0]
    elif len(sorted_candidates) == 2:
        fracs = [0.62, 0.38]
    elif len(sorted_candidates) == 3:
        fracs = [0.55, 0.29, 0.16]
    else:
        fracs = [0.50, 0.30, 0.13, 0.07]

    for i, (p, sc, lab) in enumerate(sorted_candidates):
        rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if rem < 25.0:
            break
        alloc = first_budget * fracs[min(i, len(fracs) - 1)]
        alloc = min(alloc, rem - 10.0)
        if alloc < 25.0:
            alloc = max(25.0, rem - 10.0)

        p2, sc2 = polish_sequence(p, alloc, rng, f"{lab}_{i}")
        if sc2 < global_best_score - 1e-12:
            global_best_score = sc2
            global_best_p = p2.copy()
            print(f"Phase update: {lab} -> {global_best_score:.8f}")

    # ----------------------- larger-n exploration -----------------------
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 220.0 and global_best_p.size < 13500:
        n_large = int(min(MAX_OPT_N, max(2048, global_best_p.size * 1.22) + 1))
        if n_large > global_best_p.size:
            p_large = resize_p(global_best_p, n_large)
            # Small random smoothing helps interpolation bins.
            p_large = random_perturb_p(p_large, rng, mode=0, scale=0.02)
            slice_s = min(rem * 0.28, 520.0)
            p2, sc2 = polish_sequence(p_large, slice_s, rng, f"large_n_{n_large}")
            if sc2 < global_best_score - 1e-12:
                global_best_score = sc2
                global_best_p = p2.copy()
                print(f"Large-n update: n={n_large}, score={global_best_score:.8f}")

    # ----------------------- final global polish -----------------------
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 80.0:
        slice_s = rem * 0.72
        p2, sc2 = polish_sequence(global_best_p, slice_s, rng, "final_global")
        if sc2 < global_best_score - 1e-12:
            global_best_score = sc2
            global_best_p = p2.copy()

    # One last deterministic LSE/L-BFGS if still time.
    rem = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if rem > 40.0 and global_best_p.size <= 18000:
        p2, sc2 = run_lbfgs_lse(global_best_p, rem * 0.9)
        if sc2 < global_best_score - 1e-12:
            global_best_score = sc2
            global_best_p = p2.copy()

    # ----------------------- final oracle verification -----------------------
    # We keep the internal optimum unless evaluate_sequence says another
    # retained candidate is better. This protects against minor internal
    # numerical discrepancies.
    final_p = global_best_p.copy()
    final_eval = np.inf

    try:
        out = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in final_p]
        if len(out) > 0 and sum(out) > 1e-8 and len(out) <= 13000:
            ev = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
            if np.isfinite(ev):
                final_eval = ev
    except Exception:
        final_eval = np.inf

    if parent is not None and parent.size <= 13000 and parent.size != final_p.size:
        try:
            out = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in parent]
            ev = float(evaluate_sequence(out))
            if np.isfinite(ev) and ev < final_eval:
                final_eval = ev
                final_p = parent.copy()
        except Exception:
            pass

    if not np.isfinite(final_eval):
        final_eval = global_best_score

    out = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in final_p]
    if not out or sum(out) < 0.01:
        out = [0.5] * 64

    elapsed = time.monotonic() - t_start
    print("=" * 60)
    print(f"FINAL internal best = {global_best_score:.10f}, oracle = {final_eval:.10f}")
    print(f"Returned length = {len(out)}, total time = {elapsed:.1f}s / {total_budget:.1f}s")
    print("=" * 60)

    return out
```
